<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the packet form of the higher-dimensional Duke equidistribution problem for totally real fields of any fixed prime degree at least five, allowing arbitrary local homothety types of full lattices. As the multiplier-order discriminant tends to infinity, the volume-weighted packet measures converge to Haar probability measure, with no escape of mass.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every fixed prime $n\ge5$ and every sequence $(K_i,M_i,\sigma_i)$ as above with $D(M_i)\to\infty$, $$\mu_{K_i,M_i,\sigma_i}\longrightarrow m_n.$$ The convergence is weak convergence of probability measures. In particular, it holds against every continuous compactly supported function, and no mass escapes into the cusp.
  - Explanation: Establishes the packet form of the higher-dimensional Duke equidistribution theorem for totally real number fields of fixed prime degree at least five: as the multiplier-order discriminant grows without bound, the volume-weighted packet probability measures of compact diagonal orbits converge weakly to the invariant Haar probability measure on the space of unimodular lattices with no escape of mass into the cusp.
- **C2** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-packet-model)): The maps $$(N,t)\longmapsto (Q\mathop{\mathrm{N}}I)^{-1/n}\sigma(N)t \qquad (N\in\mathcal N(I),\ t\in C)$$ induce a bijection from the disjoint union of the quotients by $$\epsilon:(N,t)\longmapsto (\epsilon N,\sigma(\epsilon)^{-1}t),\qquad \epsilon\in E,$$ onto the union of the packet orbits. Counting measure on the $N$ labels times the stated measure on $C$ descends to the sum of the orbit Haar measures. In particular, the packet probability is obtained by averaging uniformly over $I$ and $\mathcal N(I)$ and integrating over $F$ with measure $dt/\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(F)$.
  - Explanation: Provides an explicit geometric parametrization of the union of packet orbits as a disjoint union of unit-group quotients over ordinary ideal class representatives and finite local module choices, identifying the volume-weighted packet probability measure with the uniform average over ideal classes and finite local choices integrated over a fundamental domain for the unit group.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-unfolding)): Writing $\mu_*=\mu_{K,M,\sigma}$, one has $$\label{eq:unfolding} \int_{X_n}E_f\,d\mu_* =\frac1{\sqrt D\,\kappa_K} \sum_{\mathfrak a\subset R,\ \mathfrak a\ne0} P(\mathfrak a)\mathcal V_f(\mathop{\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak a/Q).$$ The identity holds for nonnegative extended integrals.
  - Explanation: Establishes the exact Hecke unfolding identity expressing the packet integral of the lattice-vector sum of any nonnegative Borel function as a sum over nonzero integral ideals of the maximal order, weighted by local containment probabilities and the product-fiber integral at the normalized ideal norm.
- **C4** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-local-moment)): For the weights just defined, $$\label{eq:shell-sum} \sum_{\mathbf k}\frac{P(\mathbf k)}{d(\mathbf k)}=\frac{Z_S}{q}.$$ If $s=1/(2n)$, then, for every $\varepsilon>0$, $$\label{eq:weight-moment} \sum_{\mathbf k}P(\mathbf k)d(\mathbf k)^{s-1} \ll_{n,\varepsilon}\frac{q^\varepsilon}{q}.$$ In particular, $$\label{eq:weight-tail} \sum_{d(\mathbf k)>Q^{1/8}}\frac{P(\mathbf k)}{d(\mathbf k)} \ll_n \frac1q Q^{-s/16}.$$
  - Explanation: Proves three uniform properties of the local containment probabilities across valuation shells: an exact total mass identity relating the norm-weighted shell sum to the local Euler factor divided by the saturation index, a small positive moment bound for local norms, and a power-saving tail estimate when the local norm exceeds a fractional power of the counting scale.
- **C5** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-residue)): For $D$ sufficiently large in terms of $n$, $$\label{eq:residue} h\zeta_K(1+h)\asymp_n\kappa_K \quad\left(0<h\le\frac4{\log D}\right), \qquad \kappa_K^{-1}\ll_n\log D.$$ For the remaining fields, the first comparison holds uniformly for sufficiently small positive $h$, and $\kappa_K^{-1}\ll_n1$.
  - Explanation: Establishes that the perturbed Dedekind zeta value near one is comparable to its residue and bounds the inverse residue by the logarithm of the field discriminant, using Stark zero-free regions and the fact that odd prime degree rules out quadratic subfields and exceptional real zeros.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-weighted-count)): Fix $A_0\ge1$ and $0<\eta\le1$. For $Q$ sufficiently large in terms of $n,A_0,\eta$, $$\label{eq:weighted-count} \sum_{x-y<\mathop{\mathrm{N}}\mathfrak a\le x}P(\mathfrak a) \ll_n \frac{\kappa_Ky}{q} \qquad (Q^{3/4}\le x\le A_0Q,\quad \eta x\le y\le x).$$ The implied constant is independent of $A_0$ and $\eta$.
  - Explanation: Combines the local shell summation identity, power-saving local norm tail bounds, the Dedekind zeta residue comparison, and Shiu short-interval theorem for multiplicative functions to bound the local-weight sum of integral ideals in short norm intervals proportionally to the zeta residue times the interval length divided by the saturation index.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-tightness)): Every sequence of the packet probabilities with $Q\to\infty$ is tight. More precisely, for $0<\theta<1$, $$\label{eq:cusp} \limsup_{Q\to\infty}\mu_* \{\Lambda:\exists\,0\ne v\in\Lambda,\ \\|v\\|_\infty<\theta\} \le C_n\theta^n.$$ Every subsequential probability limit is $A_n$-invariant.
  - Explanation: Applies the unfolded ideal sum and short-interval weighted count to indicator functions of small cubes around the origin, bounding the asymptotic packet measure of lattices with short nonzero vectors and thereby establishing tightness and diagonal invariance of subsequential weak limits via Mahler compactness criterion.
- **C8** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-ball-bound)): Let $\mu$ be a weak limit of packet probabilities along $Q\to\infty$. For every compact $\Omega\subset X_n$ there are $C_\Omega,r_\Omega>0$ such that $$\label{eq:ball-bound} \mu(xB(r))\le C_\Omega r^n \qquad(x\in\Omega,\ 0<r<r_\Omega).$$
  - Explanation: Proves that any weak subsequential limit of packet probability measures assigns mass at most proportional to the radius to the power of the degree to small ordinary balls around centers in any compact subset of the lattice space, using small boxes around lattice vectors with all coordinates nonzero.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-entropy-from-balls)): Let $n\ge3$ and let $\mu$ be an $A_n$-invariant probability on $X_n$. Suppose that for every compact $\Omega\subset X_n$ there are $C_\Omega,r_\Omega>0$ such that $$\mu(xB(r))\le C_\Omega r^n \qquad(x\in\Omega,\ 0<r<r_\Omega).$$ Then every $A_n$-invariant probability $\xi$ satisfying $\xi\le c\mu$ for some finite $c$ has $h_\xi(a(1))\ge\lambda/3$.
  - Explanation: Uses the dimension gap between the ambient ball volume exponent and the diagonal subgroup dimension to cover two-sided Bowen tubes by ordinary balls, concluding via the tube entropy criterion that any invariant probability measure dominated by a finite scalar multiple of the limit measure has strictly positive directional entropy.
- **C10** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-entropy-components)): Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:entropy-from-balls" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:entropy-from-balls">9</a>, almost every $A_n$-ergodic component of $\mu$ has positive entropy for $a(1)$.
  - Explanation: Applies ergodic decomposition and Rokhlin integral representation of metric entropy over a fixed invariant partition to deduce that the collection of zero-entropy ergodic components must have measure zero, since averaging over a positive-weight zero-entropy set would yield a dominated invariant probability violating the lower entropy bound.
- **C11** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-adelic-packet)): The adelic torus quotient is compact. Its packet and canonical probability agree with $\mathcal P_{K,M(g_f),\sigma'}$ and $\mu_{K,M(g_f),\sigma'}$ for an ordering $\sigma'$ of the real embeddings. Every prescribed local homothety type occurs this way. Changing the rational basis or the projective lifts does not change the packet; changing the allowed real diagonalizer only reorders the embeddings. Thus Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> includes every such adelic choice.
  - Explanation: Identifies the projection of the adelic algebraic torus quotient and its pushforward Haar probability measure with the combinatorial lattice packet and volume-weighted packet probability measure for arbitrary local homothety types, proving invariance under rational basis changes and projective matrix lifts.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Unimodular lattice space and diagonal subgroup** ([source](#sec:intro)): Fix a prime $n\ge5$, and write $$X_n=\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_n(\mathbb Z)\backslash\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_n(\mathbb R),\qquad A_n=\{\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(e^{u_1},\ldots,e^{u_n}):\textstyle\sum_j u_j=0\}.$$
- **Full lattice in a totally real number field** ([source](#sec:intro)): Let $K$ be a totally real field of degree $n$, let $\sigma=(\sigma_1,\ldots,\sigma_n)$ order its real embeddings, and let $M\subset K$ be a free rank-$n$ $\mathbb Z$-submodule spanning $K$ over $\mathbb Q$. We call such an $M$ a full lattice.
- **Multiplier order and lattice discriminant** ([source](#sec:intro)): Its multiplier order and discriminant are $$\mathcal O(M)=\{\alpha\in K:\alpha M\subset M\},\qquad D(M)=\|\mathop{\mathrm{Disc}}(K)\|[\mathcal O_K:\mathcal O(M)]^2.$$
- **Local homothety of full lattices** ([source](#sec:intro)): Two such lattices are *locally homothetic* if $M'_\ell=c_\ell M_\ell$ for every rational prime $\ell$, where $c_\ell\in(K\otimes_\mathbb Q\mathbb Q_\ell)^\times$ and subscripts denote completion.
- **Packet of compact diagonal orbits** ([source](#sec:intro)): The packet $\mathcal P_{K,M,\sigma}$ consists of the distinct orbits $\Lambda_{M',\sigma}wA_n$, with $$w\in W_n=\{\mathop{\mathrm{diag}}(\epsilon_1,\ldots,\epsilon_n): \epsilon_j\in\{\pm1\},\ \textstyle\prod_j\epsilon_j=1\}.$$
- **Volume-weighted packet probability measure** ([source](#sec:intro)): Set $$\label{eq:packet-probability} \mu_{K,M,\sigma} =\frac{\sum_{O\in\mathcal P_{K,M,\sigma}}\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(O)\nu_O} {\sum_{O\in\mathcal P_{K,M,\sigma}}\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(O)}.$$
- **Saturation index and counting scale** ([source](#pm-h-8)): Let $J_0=RM$, $q=[J_0:M]$, and $Q=q\sqrt D$.
- **Local containment probability** ([source](#pm-h-10)): For a tuple $\mathbf k_\ell=(k_{\mathfrak p})_{\mathfrak p\mid\ell}$ of nonnegative integers, define $$P_\ell(\mathbf k_\ell) =\Pr_{z\in R_\ell^\times}\{x\in zL_\ell\},$$ where $x$ has those component valuations and the probability is normalized unit Haar measure.
- **Product-fiber integral** ([source](#pm-h-10)): The product-fiber integral below integrates $f$ over the $C$-orbit with absolute coordinate product $z$, using logarithmic Haar measure and summing over all sign components: $$\label{eq:product-fiber} \mathcal V_f(z)=\sum_{\omega\in\{\pm1\}^n} \int_{\mathbb R^{n-1}} f\left(\omega_1e^{u_1},\ldots,\omega_{n-1}e^{u_{n-1}}, \omega_n z e^{-\sum_{j<n}u_j}\right) \,du_1\cdots\,du_{n-1},\qquad z>0.$$
- **Diagonal flow generator and root gap** ([source](#sec:entropy)): Fix distinct real numbers $\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_n$ with sum zero and put $$a(t)=\operatorname{diag}(e^{\lambda_1t},\ldots,e^{\lambda_nt}), \qquad \lambda=\min_{i\ne j}\|\lambda_i-\lambda_j\|>0.$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **S1** [claim](#stmt-lem-packet-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-packet-model): Parametrize global homothety classes in a local homothety packet via ordinary ideal class representatives $I$, finite local module choices $\mathcal N(I)$, and a fundamental domain $F$ for the unit group $E$ in $C$, using the analytic class-number formula. -> Bijection from unit quotients onto the packet orbits and expression of the packet probability as a uniform average over $I$ and $\mathcal N(I)$ integrated over $F$ with measure $dt/\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}(F)$.
- **S2** [claim](#stmt-prop-unfolding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-unfolding): Apply Hecke unfolding to the packet integral of $E_f(\Lambda)=\sum_{0\ne v\in\Lambda}f(v)$ by grouping nonzero elements $\alpha\in I\setminus\{0\}$ into free $E$-orbits corresponding to integral ideals in the inverse class and integrating over $C$ via Tonelli. -> Exact unfolding identity expressing the packet integral of $E_f(\Lambda)=\sum_{0\ne v\in\Lambda}f(v)$ as a sum over nonzero integral ideals of $R$ weighted by local containment probabilities and the product-fiber integral.
- **S3** [claim](#stmt-lem-local-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-local-moment): Compute the total shell weight sum using additive Haar measure on $R_\ell$ and transitive unit action on valuation shells, then use a unit in each component projection of $L_\ell$ to bound component valuation tails and factor the moment over $S$. -> Exact shell normalization, local norm moment bound for $s=1/(2n)$, and power-saving tail bound over large local norms.
- **S4** [claim](#stmt-lem-residue) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-residue): Combine Stark exceptional-zero lemmas (excluding real exceptional zeros because odd prime degree $n$ admits no quadratic subfield) with the Hadamard logarithmic derivative identity for the completed Dedekind zeta function. -> Uniform residue comparison for the Dedekind zeta function near one and logarithmic upper bound on the inverse residue.
- **S5** [claim](#stmt-prop-weighted-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-weighted-count): Factor ideal norms into $S$-supported valuation shells and coprime parts, bound the coprime sum on $d=d(\mathbf k)\le Q^{1/8}$ via Shiu theorem in Pollack uniform formulation and the Euler factor comparison, whereby the local Euler product cancels between the removed Euler factors and the local shell sum, and bound $d>Q^{1/8}$ via the local weight tail. -> Short-interval weighted ideal bound proportional to the Dedekind zeta residue times the interval length divided by $q$.
- **S6** [claim](#stmt-prop-tightness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-tightness): Evaluate the product-fiber integral for the cube indicator $f=\mathbf 1_{[-\theta,\theta]^n}$, apply the unfolding formula and integration by parts with the weighted count above $U=Q^{3/4}$, and invoke Mahler compactness criterion. -> Cusp mass bound $O_n(\theta^n)$, tightness of the packet probabilities along $Q\to\infty$, and $A_n$-invariance of subsequential weak limits.
- **S7** [claim](#stmt-prop-ball-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ball-bound): Select at each lattice $x\in\Omega$ a vector $w_x$ with all coordinates nonzero via a finite open cover, bound the packet expectation of small boxes $w_x+[-b_0r,b_0r]^n$ by $O(r^n)$ using the unfolding identity and short-interval weighted count, and pass to the weak limit on open balls $xB(r)$ via Portmanteau theorem. -> Ordinary ball mass bound on $xB(r)$ for $x\in\Omega$ and $0<r<r_\Omega$.
- **S8** [claim](#stmt-prop-entropy-from-balls) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-entropy-from-balls): Cover the two-sided Bowen tube by translates of ordinary balls with $r=e^{-\lambda t}$, combine with the $O(r^n)$ ball mass bound to obtain exponential tube decay for any dominated probability, and apply the tube entropy criterion. -> Positive directional entropy lower bound $h_\xi(a(1))\ge\lambda/3$ for every $A_n$-invariant probability $\xi$ satisfying $\xi\le c\mu$.
- **S9** [claim](#stmt-lem-entropy-components) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-entropy-components): Decompose $\mu$ into $A_n$-ergodic components and apply Rokhlin entropy integral formula over the $a(1)$-fixed component partition; if the zero-entropy set $Z_0=\{z:h_{\nu_z}(T)=0\}$ had positive weight $w=\tau(Z_0)>0$, the normalized restriction $\xi\le w^{-1}\mu$ would have $h_\xi(T)=0$, contradicting Proposition 9. -> Almost every $A_n$-ergodic component of $\mu$ has positive entropy for $a(1)$.
- **S10** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-entropy-components-2): Use $D(M)\le Q^{2n}$ to deduce $Q_i\to\infty$, extract tight $A_n$-invariant subsequential limits $\mu$, apply Einsiedler-Katok-Lindenstrauss prime-degree measure classification to conclude every positive-entropy $A_n$-ergodic component equals $m_n$, and integrate components to obtain $\mu_i\to m_n$. -> Weak convergence of packet probabilities to $m_n$ with no escape of mass.
- **S11** [claim](#stmt-prop-adelic-packet) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-adelic-packet): Identify the projective adelic torus quotient with the idelic quotient, compute the finite idele stabilizer of the completed lattice, and match the fiber decomposition over ideal classes with Lemma 2. -> Compactness of the adelic torus quotient and exact agreement of its pushforward Haar probability with $\mu_{K,M(g_f),\sigma'}$ independently of rational basis and projective lifts.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **P3-I1**: For every fixed prime degree at least five and any sequence of totally real fields, full lattices of a fixed local homothety type, and ordered real embeddings whose multiplier-order discriminants tend to infinity, the volume-weighted packet probability measures converge weakly to the unimodular Haar probability measure with no escape of mass into the cusp.
- **P3-I2**: The packet integral of any nonnegative Borel lattice-sum function unfolds exactly into a sum over nonzero integral ideals of the maximal order, weighted by local containment probabilities and the product-fiber integral at the normalized ideal norm.
- **P3-I3**: For any normalized local lattice whose maximal-order span equals the full local ring, the valuation-shell containment probabilities sum when weighted by reciprocal local norms to the local Euler factor divided by the saturation index, while each component valuation satisfies an exponential tail bound.
- **P3-I4**: Every diagonal-invariant probability measure on the unimodular lattice space satisfying a degree-n polynomial mass bound on small ordinary balls forces positive directional metric entropy at least one-third of the minimal root separation on every dominated invariant probability measure, and hence on almost every ergodic component.
- **P3-I5**: The adelic torus quotient is compact, and pushing forward its canonical Haar probability measure produces the exact combinatorial volume-weighted packet probability measure independently of rational basis choices and projective matrix lifts.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **P3-A1**: Group orbits solely by a shared multiplier order rather than by a single local homothety type. -> A single multiplier order can combine multiple distinct local homothety packets, whereas the equidistribution theorem and unfolding identity are formulated for one complete local homothety type at a time.
- **P3-A2**: Invoke Theorem 1 to claim equidistribution for an individual periodic torus orbit, to extract an effective convergence rate, or to treat composite field degrees. -> An individual ideal class or single orbit is not averaged by the packet measure, Stark's exceptional-zero exclusion relies on odd degree to avoid quadratic subfields, and the measure classification step requires prime degree.
- **P3-A3**: Assume that the finite local choice count T equals the saturation index q or that local module projections across prime components are independent or invertible. -> Equating T with q miscalculates the packet normalization, and assuming component independence or module invertibility restricts the proof away from arbitrary noninvertible local modules.
- **P3-A4**: Establish positive metric entropy only for the weak limit measure itself rather than for every invariant probability measure dominated by a finite scalar multiple of the limit. -> Positive entropy of the limit measure alone permits a positive-weight family of zero-entropy ergodic components, which blocks the measure classification theorem from identifying every component as Haar measure.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Constructive finite local valuation-shell and tail calculation in Lemma 4 only: for a finite quotient module L_ell / ell^m R_ell inside (Z/ell^m Z)^n, compute the saturation index q_ell, exact shell containment probabilities P_ell(k), the exact shell-sum identity sum P(k)/d(k) = Z_ell / q_ell, and the componentwise valuation tail bound Pr(v_p >= k) <= ell^(-k) under the full-span condition R_ell L_ell = R_ell. The global weak equidistribution theorem (Theorem 1) is an asymptotic measure-classification result with no finite input algorithm and is not certified by this code.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with prime ell, truncation exponent m, and generator vectors gens in Z^n.
# PRECONDITIONS: ell in {2, 3, 5}, 1 <= m <= 2, 1 <= n <= 3, gens non-empty list of n-tuples.
# OUTPUTS: JSON object with q_ell, full_span, shell_sum, target_ratio, identity_holds, tail_bound_holds.
# TIME: O(ell^(n*m) * len(gens) * n) finite group generation and shell summation steps.
# SPACE: O(ell^(n*m) * n) exact integer tuples in the quotient module (Z/ell^m Z)^n.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic via python-flint fmpq; no floating-point operations.
# FAILURE: Raises AssertionError or KeyError on invalid input parameters or malformed vectors.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpq

data = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())
ell, m, gens = data["ell"], data["m"], data["gens"]
assert ell in (2, 3, 5) and m in (1, 2) and len(gens) >= 1
n = len(gens[0])
assert 1 <= n <= 3 and all(len(g) == n for g in gens)
mod = ell ** m

sub = {tuple([0] * n)}
for g in gens:
    nxt = set()
    for c in range(mod):
        gv = tuple((c * x) % mod for x in g)
        for s in sub:
            nxt.add(tuple((s[i] + gv[i]) % mod for i in range(n)))
    sub = nxt

card = len(sub)
q_ell = (mod ** n) // card
full_span = all(any(x[i] % ell != 0 for x in sub) for i in range(n))

def v_ell(val):
    if val % mod == 0:
        return m
    v = 0
    while val % ell == 0:
        val //= ell
        v += 1
    return v

shells = {}
for x in sub:
    t = tuple(v_ell(xi) for xi in x)
    shells[t] = shells.get(t, 0) + 1

z_ell = fmpq(ell, ell - 1) ** n
shell_sum = fmpq(0)
for t, count in shells.items():
    shell_card = fmpq(1)
    weight_factor = fmpq(1)
    for ti in t:
        if ti < m:
            shell_card *= (ell ** (m - ti)) * fmpq(ell - 1, ell)
            weight_factor *= fmpq(1, ell ** ti)
        else:
            weight_factor *= fmpq(1, ell ** m) * fmpq(ell, ell - 1)
    p_t = fmpq(count, int(shell_card))
    shell_sum += p_t * weight_factor

tail_ok = True
for i in range(n):
    for k in range(1, m + 1):
        pk = ell ** k
        hit = sum(1 for x in sub if x[i] % pk == 0)
        if fmpq(hit, card) > fmpq(1, pk):
            tail_ok = False

target_ratio = z_ell / fmpq(q_ell)
out = {
    "full_span": bool(full_span),
    "identity_holds": bool(shell_sum == target_ratio),
    "q_ell": int(q_ell),
    "shell_sum": str(shell_sum),
    "tail_bound_holds": bool(tail_ok),
    "target_ratio": str(target_ratio),
}
print(json.dumps(out, sort_keys=True))
```

In particular, it holds against every continuous compactly supported function, and no mass escapes into the cusp.",

```json
    "oracle_code": "import itertools, json, sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\ntry:\n    payload = json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))\n    prime = payload[\"ell\"]\n    depth = payload[\"m\"]\n    basis = payload[\"gens\"]\nexcept Exception:\n    sys.exit(2)\n\nif prime not in (2, 3, 5) or depth not in (1, 2) or not basis:\n    sys.exit(3)\ndim = len(basis[0])\nif not (1 <= dim <= 3) or any(len(row) != dim for row in basis):\n    sys.exit(4)\n\nmodulus = prime ** depth\nambient_size = modulus ** dim\n\n# Independent idiom: Cartesian coefficient span over Z/modulus Z\ncoeffs = itertools.product(range(modulus), repeat=len(basis))\nelements = set()\nfor comb in coeffs:\n    vec = [0] * dim\n    for c, row in zip(comb, basis):\n        for j in range(dim):\n            vec[j] = (vec[j] + c * row[j]) % modulus\n    elements.add(tuple(vec))\n\nindex_q = ambient_size // len(elements)\nspans_unit = True\nfor coord in range(dim):\n    if all(vec[coord] % prime == 0 for vec in elements):\n        spans_unit = False\n\n# Independent idiom: sum additive Haar masses over all ambient cosets in (Z/modulus Z)^dim\neuler_factor = Fraction(prime, prime - 1) ** dim\naccum_mass = Fraction(0)\nfor vec in itertools.product(range(modulus), repeat=dim):\n    if vec in elements:\n        accum_mass += Fraction(1, ambient_size) * euler_factor\n\nexpected_ratio = euler_factor / Fraction(index_q)\nif accum_mass != expected_ratio:\n    sys.exit(5)\n\n# Check componentwise valuation tail probabilities against 1 / prime^k\ntail_valid = True\nfor coord in range(dim):\n    for exp_k in range(1, depth + 1):\n        div_k = prime ** exp_k\n        multiples = [vec for vec in elements if vec[coord] % div_k == 0]\n        if Fraction(len(multiples), len(elements)) > Fraction(1, div_k):\n            tail_valid = False\n\nif spans_unit and not tail_valid:\n    sys.exit(6)\n\ndef fmt_frac(fr):\n    return str(fr.numerator) if fr.denominator == 1 else \"%d/%d\" % (fr.numerator, fr.denominator)\n\ntruth = {\n    \"full_span\": spans_unit,\n    \"identity_holds\": (accum_mass == expected_ratio),\n    \"q_ell\": index_q,\n    \"shell_sum\": fmt_frac(accum_mass),\n    \"tail_bound_holds\": tail_valid,\n    \"target_ratio\": fmt_frac(expected_ratio),\n}\n\ntry:\n    cand_out = json.load(open(sys.argv[2]))\n    exp_out = json.loads(sys.argv[3])\nexcept Exception:\n    sys.exit(7)\n\nif cand_out != truth or exp_out != truth:\n    sys.exit(8)\nsys.exit(0)\n",

The global weak equidistribution theorem (Theorem 1) is an asymptotic measure-classification result with no finite input algorithm and is not certified by this code.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON object with prime ell, truncation exponent m, and generator vectors gens in Z^n.\n# PRECONDITIONS: ell in {2, 3, 5}, 1 <= m <= 2, 1 <= n <= 3, gens non-empty list of n-tuples.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON object with q_ell, full_span, shell_sum, target_ratio, identity_holds, tail_bound_holds.\n# TIME: O(ell^(n*m) * len(gens) * n) finite group generation and shell summation steps.\n# SPACE: O(ell^(n*m) * n) exact integer tuples in the quotient module (Z/ell^m Z)^n.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic via python-flint fmpq; no floating-point operations.\n# FAILURE: Raises AssertionError or KeyError on invalid input parameters or malformed vectors.\nimport json, sys\nfrom flint import fmpq\n\ndata = json.loads(sys.stdin.read())\nell, m, gens = data[\"ell\"], data[\"m\"], data[\"gens\"]\nassert ell in (2, 3, 5) and m in (1, 2) and len(gens) >= 1\nn = len(gens[0])\nassert 1 <= n <= 3 and all(len(g) == n for g in gens)\nmod = ell ** m\n\nsub = {tuple([0] * n)}\nfor g in gens:\n    nxt = set()\n    for c in range(mod):\n        gv = tuple((c * x) % mod for x in g)\n        for s in sub:\n            nxt.add(tuple((s[i] + gv[i]) % mod for i in range(n)))\n    sub = nxt\n\ncard = len(sub)\nq_ell = (mod ** n) // card\nfull_span = all(any(x[i] % ell != 0 for x in sub) for i in range(n))\n\ndef v_ell(val):\n    if val % mod == 0:\n        return m\n    v = 0\n    while val % ell == 0:\n        val //= ell\n        v += 1\n    return v\n\nshells = {}\nfor x in sub:\n    t = tuple(v_ell(xi) for xi in x)\n    shells[t] = shells.get(t, 0) + 1\n\nz_ell = fmpq(ell, ell - 1) ** n\nshell_sum = fmpq(0)\nfor t, count in shells.items():\n    shell_card = fmpq(1)\n    weight_factor = fmpq(1)\n    for ti in t:\n        if ti < m:\n            shell_card *= (ell ** (m - ti)) * fmpq(ell - 1, ell)\n            weight_factor *= fmpq(1, ell ** ti)\n        else:\n            weight_factor *= fmpq(1, ell ** m) * fmpq(ell, ell - 1)\n    p_t = fmpq(count, int(shell_card))\n    shell_sum += p_t * weight_factor\n\ntail_ok = True\nfor i in range(n):\n    for k in range(1, m + 1):\n        pk = ell ** k\n        hit = sum(1 for x in sub if x[i] % pk == 0)\n        if fmpq(hit, card) > fmpq(1, pk):\n            tail_ok = False\n\ntarget_ratio = z_ell / fmpq(q_ell)\nout = {\n    \"full_span\": bool(full_span),\n    \"identity_holds\": bool(shell_sum == target_ratio),\n    \"q_ell\": int(q_ell),\n    \"shell_sum\": str(shell_sum),\n    \"tail_bound_holds\": bool(tail_ok),\n    \"target_ratio\": str(target_ratio),\n}\nprint(json.dumps(out, sort_keys=True))\n",
```

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-01** (hypothesis): The main equidistribution theorem applies only to totally real number fields of fixed prime degree at least five; composite degrees are excluded because both the exceptional-zero descent without quadratic subfields and the measure-classification step rely on primality. Blocked use: ['Applying the unconditional equidistribution result to number fields of composite degree or degree below five.']
- **limit-02** (hypothesis): The ambient field must be totally real so that its real embeddings diagonalize the torus action and its roots of unity are strictly plus or minus one; fields with complex embeddings are outside this regime. Blocked use: ['Transferring the packet equidistribution theorem to number fields with complex places.']
- **limit-03** (formal_scope): Equidistribution is proved for the volume-weighted average over a complete local homothety packet of orbits, not for an individual periodic torus orbit or a single ideal class. Blocked use: ['Asserting equidistribution for a single periodic diagonal orbit or an individual ideal class without averaging over the complete local homothety packet.']
- **limit-04** (hypothesis): A packet is determined by a prescribed local homothety type across all rational primes; grouping solely by the multiplier order is coarser and can combine several distinct packets. Blocked use: ['Identifying a multiplier order with a single local homothety packet when local module completions are not locally homothetic.']
- **limit-05** (nonconstructive): The convergence to Haar probability measure is qualitative weak convergence as the multiplier-order discriminant tends to infinity; no effective rate of convergence or explicit error term in the counting scale is established. Blocked use: ['Using the theorem to bound the finite-discriminant discrepancy or convergence rate of packet averages.']
- **limit-06** (hypothesis): Uniform local moment and valuation-tail bounds require each local module completion to have full maximal-order span; modules whose maximal-order span is a proper sub-ideal before saturation normalization do not satisfy the unit-projection condition directly. Blocked use: ['Applying the unnormalized local valuation-tail inequality without first normalizing by the maximal-order span.']
- **limit-07** (edge_case): The cardinality of the finite set of local module choices in an ideal class need not equal the saturation index, and the local component projections of a normalized module need not be independent or equal to the full component integer rings. Blocked use: ['Assuming that the number of local lattice choices equals the saturation index or that local component projections factor independently.']
- **limit-08** (approximation): The short-interval weighted ideal count holds only for scales sufficiently large in terms of the degree, upper scale multiplier, and relative interval width, within the stated window of scales and interval lengths. Blocked use: ['Applying the short-interval upper bound below the implicit scale threshold or to intervals shorter than a fixed positive fraction of the endpoint.']
- **limit-09** (nonconstructive): In establishing ordinary ball mass bounds on compact sets, the pointwise choice of a lattice vector with nonzero coordinates uses a finite compactness cover and need not be continuous or measurable, and the limit bound avoids exchanging the shrinking-radius and packet limits. Blocked use: ['Treating the pointwise coordinate-avoidance vector selection as a globally continuous or measurable map or exchanging the small-radius and packet limits.']
- **limit-10** (hypothesis): The two-sided tube entropy criterion on the noncompact lattice space requires the weak limit to be a probability measure, and ruling out zero-entropy ergodic components requires establishing positive entropy for every invariant probability dominated by a finite multiple of the limit measure. Blocked use: ['Applying the tube entropy criterion when mass escapes to the cusp or deducing componentwise positive entropy from the entropy of the limit measure alone without domination.']
- **limit-11** (formal_scope): While finite p-adic valuation shell identities and local norm moments can be checked on finite splitting types, the global equidistribution theorem relies on nonconstructive compactness, ergodic decomposition, and measure-classification steps that admit no finite constructive algorithm. Blocked use: ['Interpreting finite local shell checks as an algorithmic certificate for global weak convergence or ergodic measure classification.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 015 (`lean/docs/015.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Equidistribution of Prime-Degree Torus Packets with Arbitrary Local Type](../../preprints/Equidistribution-of-Prime-Degree-Torus-Packets-with-Arbitrary-Local-Type-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves equidistribution of volume-weighted torus packets for totally real number fields of every fixed prime degree at least five. For any sequence of full lattices whose multiplier-order discriminants tend to infinity, the packet measures converge weakly to Haar probability measure and form a tight family, so no mass escapes. Arbitrary local homothety types are allowed, and the fields may vary along the sequence.

Comparator source `DukePrimeDegree.lean` (9711 bytes, sha256 `a6a7425910ce1b6b486efc1fe05d0c262c523635eeef0a14481741557a1c966d`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

section
noncomputable section
open scoped Matrix Topology BigOperators Classical TensorProduct ENNReal
open MeasureTheory Filter Set Module
namespace PrimeDegreePackets

abbrev G (n : ℕ) := Matrix.SpecialLinearGroup (Fin n) ℝ

def integralGroup (n : ℕ) : Subgroup (G n) :=
  (Matrix.SpecialLinearGroup.map (Int.castRingHom ℝ)).range

abbrev X (n : ℕ) := Quotient (QuotientGroup.rightRel (integralGroup n))

instance xMeasurableSpace (n : ℕ) : MeasurableSpace (X n) := borel (X n)
instance xBorelSpace (n : ℕ) : BorelSpace (X n) := ⟨rfl⟩

def rightAction {n : ℕ} (x : X n) (g : G n) : X n :=
  Quotient.map (fun h => h * g) (by
    intro a b hab
    change (QuotientGroup.rightRel (integralGroup n)) a b at hab
    change (QuotientGroup.rightRel (integralGroup n)) (a * g) (b * g)
    rw [QuotientGroup.rightRel_apply] at hab ⊢
    simpa only [mul_inv_rev, mul_assoc, mul_inv_cancel_left] using hab) x

@[simp] theorem rightAction_one {n : ℕ} (x : X n) : rightAction x 1 = x := by
  induction x using Quotient.inductionOn with
  | h a => exact congrArg (Quotient.mk _) (mul_one a)

@[simp] theorem rightAction_mul {n : ℕ} (x : X n) (g h : G n) :
    rightAction (rightAction x g) h = rightAction x (g * h) := by
  induction x using Quotient.inductionOn with
  | h a => exact congrArg (Quotient.mk _) (mul_assoc a g h)

def signMatrices (n : ℕ) : Set (G n) :=
  {g | (∀ i j, i ≠ j → g.1 i j = 0) ∧ ∀ i, g.1 i i = 1 ∨ g.1 i i = -1}

def rowLattice {n : ℕ} (g : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℝ) : Submodule ℤ (Fin n → ℝ) :=
  Submodule.span ℤ (Set.range fun i => g i)

structure FullLattice (n : ℕ) (K : Type*) [Field K] [NumberField K] where
  carrier : Submodule ℤ K
  basis : Basis (Fin n) ℤ carrier
  spans : Submodule.span ℚ (carrier : Set K) = ⊤

def multiplierOrder {n : ℕ} {K : Type*} [Field K] [NumberField K] (M : FullLattice n K) : AddSubgroup K where
  carrier := {a | ∀ x ∈ M.carrier, a * x ∈ M.carrier}
  zero_mem' := by simp
  add_mem' := by
    intro a b ha hb x hx
    simpa only [add_mul] using M.carrier.add_mem (ha x hx) (hb x hx)
  neg_mem' := by
    intro a ha x hx
    simpa only [neg_mul] using M.carrier.neg_mem (ha x hx)

def integralElements (K : Type*) [Field K] : AddSubgroup K :=
  (integralClosure ℤ K).toSubring.toAddSubgroup

def multiplierDiscriminant {n : ℕ} {K : Type*} [Field K] [NumberField K]
    (M : FullLattice n K) : ℝ :=
  |(NumberField.discr K : ℝ)| *
    ((multiplierOrder M).relIndex (integralElements K) : ℝ) ^ 2

abbrev OrderedEmbeddings (n : ℕ) (K : Type*) [Field K] := Fin n ≃ (K →+* ℝ)

def embeddingMatrix {n : ℕ} {K : Type*} [Field K] [NumberField K]
    (M : FullLattice n K) (σ : OrderedEmbeddings n K) : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℝ :=
  fun i j => σ j (M.basis i)

def normalizedLattice {n : ℕ} {K : Type*} [Field K] [NumberField K]
    (M : FullLattice n K) (σ : OrderedEmbeddings n K) : Submodule ℤ (Fin n → ℝ) :=
  let B := embeddingMatrix M σ
  rowLattice (|(Matrix.det B)| ^ (-(1 : ℝ) / n) • B)

def latticePoint {n : ℕ} {K : Type*} [Field K] [NumberField K]
    (M : FullLattice n K) (σ : OrderedEmbeddings n K) : X n :=
  if h : ∃ g : G n, rowLattice g.1 = normalizedLattice M σ then
    Quotient.mk _ h.choose
  else Quotient.mk _ (1 : G n)

abbrev LogCoordinates (d : ℕ) := Fin d → ℝ

def diagonalFlow {d : ℕ} (u : LogCoordinates d) : G (d + 1) :=
  ⟨Matrix.diagonal (Fin.snoc (fun i => Real.exp (u i)) (Real.exp (-∑ i, u i))), by
    rw [Matrix.det_diagonal, Fin.prod_univ_castSucc]
    simp only [Fin.snoc_castSucc, Fin.snoc_last]
    rw [← Real.exp_sum, ← Real.exp_add, add_neg_cancel, Real.exp_zero]⟩

@[simp] theorem diagonalFlow_zero (d : ℕ) : diagonalFlow (0 : LogCoordinates d) = 1 := by
  apply Subtype.ext
  change Matrix.diagonal _ = (1 : Matrix (Fin (d + 1)) (Fin (d + 1)) ℝ)
  simp
  ext i
  refine Fin.lastCases ?_ (fun j => ?_) i <;> simp

@[simp] theorem diagonalFlow_add {d : ℕ} (u v : LogCoordinates d) :
    diagonalFlow (u + v) = diagonalFlow u * diagonalFlow v := by
  apply Subtype.ext
  change Matrix.diagonal _ = Matrix.diagonal _ * Matrix.diagonal _
  rw [Matrix.diagonal_mul_diagonal]
  congr 1
  funext i
  refine Fin.lastCases ?_ (fun j => ?_) i
  · simp [Real.exp_add, Finset.sum_add_distrib, neg_add_rev, mul_comm]
  · simp [Real.exp_add]

def orbitMap {d : ℕ} (x : X (d + 1)) (u : LogCoordinates d) : X (d + 1) :=
  rightAction x (diagonalFlow u)

@[simp] theorem orbitMap_zero {d : ℕ} (x : X (d + 1)) : orbitMap x 0 = x := by
  simp [orbitMap]

@[simp] theorem orbitMap_add {d : ℕ} (x : X (d + 1)) (u v : LogCoordinates d) :
    orbitMap x (u + v) = orbitMap (orbitMap x u) v := by
  simp [orbitMap]

def periodGroup {d : ℕ} (x : X (d + 1)) : AddSubgroup (LogCoordinates d) where
  carrier := {u | orbitMap x u = x}
  zero_mem' := orbitMap_zero x
  add_mem' := by
    intro u v hu hv
    change orbitMap x (u + v) = x
    rw [orbitMap_add, hu, hv]
  neg_mem' := by
    intro u hu
    have h := congrArg (fun y => orbitMap y (-u)) hu
    rw [← orbitMap_add, add_neg_cancel, orbitMap_zero] at h
    exact h.symm

def diagonalOrbit {d : ℕ} (x : X (d + 1)) : Set (X (d + 1)) := Set.range (orbitMap x)

def orbitVolume {d : ℕ} (x : X (d + 1)) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  addCovolume (periodGroup x) (LogCoordinates d) volume

def orbitalHaar {d : ℕ} (x : X (d + 1)) : Measure (X (d + 1)) :=
  if h : HasAddFundamentalDomain (periodGroup x) (LogCoordinates d) volume then
    Measure.map (orbitMap x) (volume.restrict h.ExistsIsAddFundamentalDomain.choose)
  else 0

def orbitProbability {d : ℕ} (x : X (d + 1)) : Measure (X (d + 1)) :=
  (orbitVolume x)⁻¹ • orbitalHaar x

abbrev LocalAlgebra (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] (K : Type*) [Field K] [NumberField K] :=
  Padic p ⊗[ℚ] K

def completedLattice {n : ℕ} {K : Type*} [Field K] [NumberField K]
    (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime] (M : FullLattice n K) :
    Submodule (PadicInt p) (LocalAlgebra p K) :=
  Submodule.span (PadicInt p)
    ((Algebra.TensorProduct.includeRight : K →ₐ[ℚ] LocalAlgebra p K) '' (M.carrier : Set K))

def LocallyHomothetic {n : ℕ} {K : Type*} [Field K] [NumberField K]
    (M M' : FullLattice n K) : Prop :=
  ∀ (p : ℕ) (hp : p.Prime), letI : Fact p.Prime := ⟨hp⟩
    ∃ c : (LocalAlgebra p K)ˣ,
      (completedLattice p M' : Set (LocalAlgebra p K)) =
        (fun z => (c : LocalAlgebra p K) * z) '' (completedLattice p M : Set (LocalAlgebra p K))

def packetOrbits {d : ℕ} {K : Type*} [Field K] [NumberField K]
    (M : FullLattice (d + 1) K) (σ : OrderedEmbeddings (d + 1) K) :
    Set (Set (X (d + 1))) :=
  {O | ∃ M' : FullLattice (d + 1) K, LocallyHomothetic M M' ∧
    ∃ w ∈ signMatrices (d + 1), O = diagonalOrbit (rightAction (latticePoint M' σ) w)}

def orbitBasepoint {d : ℕ} {K : Type*} [Field K] [NumberField K]
    {M : FullLattice (d + 1) K} {σ : OrderedEmbeddings (d + 1) K}
    (O : packetOrbits M σ) : X (d + 1) :=
  Classical.epsilon (fun x => diagonalOrbit x = O.1)

def packetMeasure {d : ℕ} {K : Type*} [Field K] [NumberField K]
    (M : FullLattice (d + 1) K) (σ : OrderedEmbeddings (d + 1) K) : Measure (X (d + 1)) :=
  (∑' O : packetOrbits M σ, orbitVolume (orbitBasepoint O))⁻¹ •
    Measure.sum (fun O : packetOrbits M σ =>
      orbitVolume (orbitBasepoint O) • orbitProbability (orbitBasepoint O))

def IsHaarProbability {n : ℕ} (μ : Measure (X n)) : Prop :=
  IsProbabilityMeasure μ ∧ ∀ g : G n, Measure.map (fun x => rightAction x g) μ = μ

def WeakProbabilityConvergence {n : ℕ} (μ : ℕ → Measure (X n)) (m : Measure (X n)) : Prop :=
  (∀ i, IsProbabilityMeasure (μ i)) ∧ IsProbabilityMeasure m ∧
    ∀ f : BoundedContinuousFunction (X n) ℝ,
    Tendsto (fun i => ∫ x, f x ∂μ i) atTop (𝓝 (∫ x, f x ∂m))

end PrimeDegreePackets
end
end

open MeasureTheory Filter
open scoped Topology
namespace DukePrimeDegree
open PrimeDegreePackets

universe u

/-- Theorem 1.1 as weak convergence of the actual complete packet measures,
including tightness of the entire family. -/
theorem prime_degree_packet_measure_equidistribution_unconditional
    (d : ℕ) (hprime : Nat.Prime (d+1)) (hfive : 5 ≤ d+1)
    (K : ℕ → Type u) [∀ i, Field (K i)] [∀ i, NumberField (K i)]
    [∀ i, NumberField.IsTotallyReal (K i)]
    (M : ∀ i, PrimeDegreePackets.FullLattice (d+1) (K i))
    (σ : ∀ i, OrderedEmbeddings (d+1) (K i))
    (hn : ∀ i, Module.finrank ℚ (K i)=d+1)
    (hD : Tendsto (fun i => PrimeDegreePackets.multiplierDiscriminant (M i)) atTop atTop) :
    ∃ ν : Measure (X (d+1)), IsHaarProbability ν ∧
      WeakProbabilityConvergence (fun i => packetMeasure (M i) (σ i)) ν ∧
      IsTightMeasureSet (Set.range (fun i => packetMeasure (M i) (σ i))) := by
  sorry

/-- The complete packet measures converge weakly to any specified Haar
probability, in the formulation by bounded continuous test functions. -/
theorem prime_degree_packet_measures_tendsto_haar_unconditional
    (d : ℕ) (hprime : Nat.Prime (d+1)) (hfive : 5 ≤ d+1)
    (K : ℕ → Type u) [∀ i, Field (K i)] [∀ i, NumberField (K i)]
    [∀ i, NumberField.IsTotallyReal (K i)]
    (M : ∀ i, PrimeDegreePackets.FullLattice (d+1) (K i))
    (σ : ∀ i, OrderedEmbeddings (d+1) (K i))
    (hn : ∀ i, Module.finrank ℚ (K i)=d+1)
    (hD : Tendsto (fun i => PrimeDegreePackets.multiplierDiscriminant (M i)) atTop atTop)
    (m : ProbabilityMeasure (X (d+1)))
    (hm : IsHaarProbability (m : Measure (X (d+1)))) :
    WeakProbabilityConvergence (fun i => packetMeasure (M i) (σ i))
      (m : Measure (X (d+1))) := by
  sorry

end DukePrimeDegree

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the packet form of the higher-dimensional Duke equidistribution problem for totally real fields of any fixed prime degree at least five, allowing arbitrary local homothety types of full lattices. As the multiplier-order discriminant tends to infinity, the volume-weighted packet measures converge to Haar probability measure, with no escape of mass.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
